# **ChainTorrent MVP**

## Problem Statement

JavaScript dependency distribution runs through a single centralized registry that is an availability chokepoint, a mutable source of truth, and an intermediary between the people who publish packages and the people who consume them. ChainTorrent's MVP replaces that path with a content-addressed swarm, a canonical on-chain identity registry, and transferable access entitlements, so that the distribution and identity model is proven with monetization out of bounds.

**Adoption runs individual developer first, and everything else follows from that.** A developer at a terminal gains cross-project reuse of whatever their machine has already fetched, swarm retrieval on popular packages, and installs that survive a registry outage. Build platforms adopt next and for their own reasons: a service running the same few thousand popular installs continuously, on always-on machines with real bandwidth, holds the ideal cache as a by-product of its own economics, and seeding that cache costs almost nothing once it exists. That makes them natural superseeders rather than participants anyone has to recruit, and it is why no privileged bootstrap host is part of this plan. CI and enterprise adoption arrive as a consequence of that sequence rather than as targets to be won early.

The sequence matters to this workplan because it determines what the MVP measures. Latency is judged against interactive local installs rather than pipeline tolerance, since a developer notices a doubled install where a pipeline does not. Cache reuse is the headline adoption metric, because it is the benefit the adopting population actually experiences. Registry-outage survival remains true throughout and is simply not the lead, since it matters most to the population that adopts last.

The design is specified in `docs/research/cryptography.md` and bounded in `docs/research/MVP Scope.md`. Undetermined decisions are held in the To-Do list below.

The cryptographic construction is specified; its research record is `docs/research/cryptography-research-notebook.md`, and its statement is `docs/research/cryptography-critical-path.md`. A validation harness on both pairing curves produces the sizes, timings, and gas against the configured piece-group size and curve defaults, and nothing waits on it: every node reads the piece-group size from the deployment record and depends only on the code it calls.

## Objectives

Deliver an install path for JavaScript dependencies that survives registry outages, serves popular packages from a peer swarm, and exercises the identity and entitlement pipeline end to end.

Prove distribution and identity. Willingness to pay and seeder compensation are out of bounds and are held in the To-Do list; the transaction flow is proven at a nominal price on assets the project publishes, as a test instrument rather than a monetization step.

Every undetermined decision is carried in the To-Do list, which every workplan that stems from this one inherits.

## Expected Outcome

A developer installs dependencies and receives a working `node_modules` assembled from a local content-addressed store, a peer swarm, or the ingest source as fallback, with canonical identity resolved on-chain and an access entitlement minted to their wallet.

* `[✅]`   `harness-crypto/verifier/evm` **The EVM concrete of the harness's verifier family, one adapter over the borrowed pairing, chain, and delivery proof: reads a statement and the proof the delivery proof family produced for it, submits them to the deployed delivery verifier as the calldata the suite's ABI description fixes, and returns the chain's verdict, with the cost of an accepted delivery carrying the estimated-fee description the chain adapter composes for the verifying transaction; the family's only module that reaches a chain; authors and binds `contracts/evm/abi/IDeliveryVerifier.json` and authors, as its producers, the verifier family's interface, selection, declared set, declared-selection builder, and mock in the family's `factory` module, beside the declaration, member identifier, and concrete settings that `harness-crypto/verifier/evm/settings` authors**

  * `[✅]`   `objective`
    * `[✅]`   Problem: the Rust reference verifier and the shipped on-chain verifier must agree on every statement and proof, and nothing yet carries a Rust statement and proof to a deployed contract or brings the contract's verdict and cost back; the suite's ABI description is the one place the contract's ABI is stated, the Rust binding is generated from that file and never copies it, and the concrete maps the proof family's own types into that ABI field by field (CD-03 cross-verification; CD-07 and AS-21's delivery cost as the estimated fee of the verifying transaction; the dependency map's `harness-crypto/verifier/evm` row and its conflict flags on the harness needing a verifier client before the chain family exists and on the code order and the runtime sequence differing; the technical requirements' harness report, whose cost fields this concrete reads; the Delivery proof and on-chain verifier milestone's exit, the Rust verifier and the deployed contract agreeing on every vector and the verifier deployed on Base Sepolia, and the Runtime sequence's first run returning the verifier-stage refusal, no deployment record configured)
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the binding of `contracts/evm/abi/IDeliveryVerifier.json` exposes `verifyMint` and `verifyTransfer`, each a `view` call over one statement struct and one proof struct returning `bool`, the member names and order those of the description
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the family's `factory` module holds the selection `VerifierConcrete` that carries a real member's admitted settings or the mock member's failure mode, the declared set `VERIFIER_CONCRETES` of real members, and the declared-selection builder `build_verifier_concrete`, so the family's selection admission, declaration lookup, composition admission, and factory take each from this node
    * `[✅]`   Non-functional: adding a further verifier concrete adds its module, its variant in `VerifierConcrete`, its entry in `VERIFIER_CONCRETES`, its arm in `build_verifier_concrete`, its variant in `VerifyOnChainErrorReturn` where its own errors reach, and its branch in the family's factory; nothing outside the family changes and no consumer names this concrete
    * `[✅]`   Functional: a statement and a proof of different relations are refused as the family's `RelationMismatch` before any call is made, an input every concrete checks
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the statement's context maps into the interface's `DeliveryContext` through each chain form's own `to_field`, its domain values through their accessors, its group elements through the pairing's `encode_g1` and `encode_g2`, and the proof's challenge and responses through `encode_scalar`, the responses in witness order, mint `alpha`, `r`, `rho`, `sigma` and transfer `x`, `y`, `s`, `rho`, `sigma`, and the second-group first messages concatenated in the form's order, empty under the both-groups form
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the chain is read in a fixed order: the verifier's code, which empty is refused as `NothingDeployed` with the address; the verifying call, whose returned `false` and whose revert are each the verdict `Rejected`; and the chain adapter's `estimate_fee` over the verifying transaction, whose refusal the concrete carries whole and whose description is the accepted delivery's cost
    * `[✅]`   Functional: a transport or JSON-RPC refusal at a read the provider serves, including a call refusal that carries no revert data, is returned whole as the concrete's own `Rpc` error, a result that does not decode as the declared return as `ResultDecode`, and a refusal of the chain adapter's fee estimation as `Fee` carrying the family's error union
    * `[✅]`   Functional: construction admits the configured verifier address through the chain forms' identity form, reads the endpoint the chain adapter's `endpoint` returns, and builds the provider over that endpoint's text and the runtime that drives it
    * `[✅]`   Non-functional: the concrete calls no factory, names no curve, address, endpoint, path, chain identifier, or version, reads no clock, and is the only harness module that names `alloy`, `tokio`, or `url`; the chain is its outer edge; the Rust binding reads `contracts/evm/abi/IDeliveryVerifier.json` and holds no second copy of its ABI; the module authors the ABI description as its first consumer and authors no contract source and no toolchain file

  * `[✅]`   `role`
    * `[✅]`   App module in the adapter role: the EVM concrete of the harness's verifier family, named for the EVM ABI it speaks, as the generate family's Solidity concrete is over the filesystem; the family's first concrete to be implemented, so it authors the family's generic interface, selection, declared set, declared-selection builder, and mock in `apps/harness-crypto/src/verifier/factory`, beside the declaration types, member identifier, and interface version `harness-crypto/verifier/evm/settings` authors there
    * `[✅]`   Owns the concrete, its constructor params, field enum, and its two errors, and the family's verdict, cost, error, and mock failure-mode types, selection, and declared set
    * `[✅]`   Maps and submits; does not build a statement or a proof, which the delivery proof family and the caller do, does not decide parity between the reference and the chain, which a caller reads from the verdicts, and does not implement the contract, which `contracts/evm/DeliveryVerifier` does, or deploy it, which `contracts/evm/deploy` does
    * `[✅]`   Does not construct an adapter, select a member from configuration, or admit a composition; the caller obtains each adapter through its family's factory, `harness-crypto/verifier/admit_verifier_selection` turns the member's identifier and untrusted table into the selection, and `harness-crypto/verifier/factory` constructs this concrete

* `[✅]`   `harness-crypto/verifier/admit_verifier_selection` **The verifier family's selection admission, in the validating form: an exhaustive branch on the member identifier its params carry narrows the untrusted settings document, the member's own table, through that member's guard, reads that member's declaration through the declaration lookup its deps carry, and calls that member's settings admission through the handle its deps carry over that declaration, a guard refusal carried intact in a variant named by the member, and returns the settings-carrying selection; creates the `admit_verifier_selection` module**

* `[✅]`   `harness-crypto/measure` **The delivery measurement, generic over the pairing, the credential KEM, the key agreement, the delivery proof with its chain forms, and the verifier concrete its caller hands it: for each configured iteration a fresh mint delivery from `harness-crypto/delivery/mint` and the transfer delivery from `harness-crypto/delivery/transfer` that follows it, untimed, and a capsule encapsulated from a drawn uniform, untimed, then only the call measured timed, `prove_mint` over a copy of the mint witnesses, `prove_transfer` over a copy of the transfer witnesses, `verify` over each relation's statement and proof, and `decapsulate` over the mint delivery's credential and identity element and the capsule, each timing the mean over the iteration count; the capsule, envelope, and each relation's proof sized in bytes by the pairing's encoders over the objects of one further mint and transfer delivery, and that pair submitted through the verifier concrete, an accepted delivery's cost carried as the verifier family's cost description; composes no delivery step, admits no context or identity, and draws only the capsule's uniform; owns the measurement, timing, size, and cost types, the refusal types, and the function's types**

  * `[✅]`   `objective`
    * `[✅]`   Problem: the piece-group size and curve defaults are adjusted from measurement, so the harness holds, for each cell it runs, the cost of every cryptographic operation a client performs and the bytes and gas of every object a settlement carries: the time of proving and verifying each relation and of decapsulating a capsule, the bytes of a capsule, an envelope, and each relation's proof, and the delivery cost of a mint and a transfer the deployed verifier accepts (CD-07 and AS-21's capsule, envelope, and proof sizes, decapsulation time per piece group, proof generation and verification time, and delivery cost for a mint and a transfer through the verifier family's cost description, which carries the chain family's estimated-fee description; RO-03's decapsulation time per group and proof generation and verification time and gas; the dependency map's `harness-crypto/measure` row, its Dependencies edges, and its conflict flags on the harness's mint and transfer each having one owner, on a measure taking its timing inputs from the delivery functions, and on a verifier client belonging to the harness's own family; the Delivery proof and on-chain verifier milestone's scope for `harness-crypto/measure`, size, timing, and gas capture per cell, and its exit, CD-07 and AS-21; `docs/research/cryptography-critical-path.md`'s costs by formula, the quantities the measurement replaces)
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the adapters, the typed mint and transfer contexts, and the admitted identity arrive handed; the function rebuilds no form of a context, admits no identity, constructs no adapter, and draws only the uniform each capsule is encapsulated from
    * `[✅]`   Functional: each iteration holds a mint delivery over the mint context and the admitted identity, a transfer delivery over the transfer context, the mint delivery's parameter set and identity element, and its recipient as the holder, and a capsule encapsulated under the mint delivery's parameter set from a uniform of the length the pairing's scalar sampling bound declares; none of the three is timed
    * `[✅]`   Functional: each of `prove_mint`, `prove_transfer`, `verify` over the mint statement and proof, `verify` over the transfer statement and proof, and `decapsulate` is timed alone, the clock read immediately before the call and immediately after it, over inputs built before the clock is read; a prover is handed copies of the witnesses its delivery returned, each secret copied through `Secret::try_new` over a clone of its exposed value, since a prover consumes what it is given
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the timing of each operation is the sum of its calls' elapsed times divided by the iteration count, so it is the mean time of one call, returned with the iteration count it was computed from
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the byte sizes are those of one mint delivery, the transfer delivery that follows it, and a capsule encapsulated under the mint delivery's parameter set, each object's group elements and scalars encoded through the pairing's `encode_g1`, `encode_g2`, and `encode_scalar` and their byte lengths summed: the capsule through `capsule_components`, the envelope the mint delivery's recipient holds through `envelope_components`, and each relation's proof through `proof_components`, its challenge, its responses, and, where the proof carries them, its second-group first messages
    * `[✅]`   Functional: that mint delivery and transfer delivery are each submitted once through the verifier concrete as `verify_on_chain` over the delivery's own statement and proof, the mint first; an accepted delivery's cost is the verifier family's cost description it was accepted at; a rejected delivery is returned with its relation; the verifier's own refusal is returned unchanged with its relation
    * `[✅]`   Functional: a failed delivery, draw, encapsulation, prove, or verification is returned unchanged in the error arm, the callee's error whole, before any later call, and a verification or submission failure is returned with the relation it concerned
    * `[✅]`   Non-functional: the function names no curve, concrete, form, scope, count, identity, endpoint, address, or context value; the iteration count arrives in its params, the adapters in its deps, and the typed contexts and the admitted identity in its payload; it reads the monotonic clock and touches no filesystem and no chain but through the verifier concrete it is handed

* `[✅]`   `harness-crypto/report` **The release-evidence report of one cell's delivery measurement: a JSON document recording the capsule, envelope, and proof sizes in bytes, the mean time of proving, verifying, and decapsulating, the delivery cost of a mint and a transfer through the verifier family's cost description, which carries the chain family's estimated-fee description, the piece-group size and primary curve defaults in force, any adjustment the data supports, and the conditions the measurement was taken under, selecting nothing against a threshold, returned and written to each destination its params carry; composes no measurement and decides no default; owns the report's text, destination, defaults, adjustment, and function types**

  * `[✅]`   `objective`
    * `[✅]`   Problem: the piece-group size and curve defaults are adjusted from measurement, and the release evidence records what was measured, the defaults in force, and any adjustment the data supports, so the harness holds, for each cell it runs, one document that states the measurement `harness-crypto/measure` returned together with the defaults and the conditions it was taken under (CD-07 and AS-21's capsule, envelope, and proof sizes, decapsulation time per piece group, proof generation and verification time, and delivery cost for a mint and a transfer through the verifier family's cost description, which carries the chain family's estimated-fee description, with the piece-group size default in force and any adjustment the measurements support recorded in release evidence; the dependency map's `harness-crypto/report` row, its Dependencies edge on `harness-crypto/measure`, and its statement that the report selects nothing against a threshold; the Delivery proof and on-chain verifier milestone's scope for `harness-crypto/report`, the release-evidence report written under the destinations its params carry, and its exit, the measurements, the piece-group size and curve defaults in force, and any adjustment the data supports recorded in release evidence; the technical requirements' Harness report, a release-evidence JSON document per cell; `docs/project-planning/success-metrics.md`'s Measurement Plan, the conditions declaring that the L1 fee is the test network's and is re-taken on the mainnet pilot; `docs/research/MVP Scope.md`'s Cost Instrumentation, the defaults adjusted when the data shows a better value and no threshold failing a run)
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the measurement, the curve's name and the cell's pairing concrete name and encoding name, the conditions, the defaults in force, and the adjustment arrive handed and admitted; the function computes, compares, and selects nothing, and records each value as it was handed
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the document is a JSON object holding the curve's name, the cell's pairing concrete name and encoding name, the conditions text, the iteration count the means were computed from, the five timings, the four sizes in bytes, the two delivery costs, the defaults in force, and the adjustment or its absence
    * `[✅]`   Functional: each timing is recorded as the whole seconds and the subsecond nanoseconds of the `Duration` the measurement holds, so no precision is lost and no conversion can fail
    * `[✅]`   Functional: each delivery's cost is recorded as the entries the cost's description carries, each entry with its name, its unit, and its quantity as decimal text, since the quantity is a `u128`, every entry the description carries recorded in its order and none invented
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the document is returned and is written, as the same text, to each destination the params carry in the order the params carry them, a file at its path or standard output; a refused write is returned unchanged with the destination it concerned, before any later destination is written
    * `[✅]`   Non-functional: the function names no curve, concrete, form, count, network, path, file name, default, or threshold; the destinations arrive in its params and everything recorded arrives in its payload; it compares no recorded value with any bound, creates no directory, and touches the filesystem and standard output only to write the document to the destinations it was handed

  * `[✅]`   `role`
    * `[✅]`   App module in the adapter role: a function the harness owns over the filesystem and standard output, as `harness-crypto/generate/solidity` is over the filesystem and `harness-crypto/measure` over the monotonic clock, beneath the crate root of `apps/harness-crypto`
    * `[✅]`   Owns `ReportText`, `ReportDestination`, `ReportDefaults`, `ReportAdjustment`, and the function's deps, params, payload, success, error, return, and signature types; consumes `DeliveryMeasurement`, `DeliveryOperationTimings`, `DeliverySizes`, and `DeliveryCosts`, which `harness-crypto/measure` owns, and `VerifyCost`, which `harness-crypto/verifier/evm` authors in the verifier family's `factory` module
    * `[✅]`   Declares the destination, defaults, adjustment, and text types as the types the configuration's admission functions admit the document's report destinations, defaults, and conditions into, and the run reads from this node to call the function once per completed cell
    * `[✅]`   Adds its module line and its re-export line to `apps/harness-crypto/src/lib.rs`, so the function is a public entry of the crate through the crate barrel
    * `[✅]`   Does not run, time, size, or submit a delivery, read the monotonic clock, or call a family's factory or the verifier
    * `[✅]`   Does not decide parity between the reference and the chain, choose a count, a destination, a default, an adjustment, a curve, or a condition, compare a value with a threshold, or adjust any default; it records the adjustment it is handed
    * `[✅]`   Does not carry a commit
